B routine priorityPublic-health news

Doctors warn of serious complications from seasonal flu - vietnamnews.vn

News Brief
Doctors in Vietnam have issued warnings about the serious complications associated with seasonal flu, particularly among children and high-risk groups. The National Children's Hospital in Hà Nội reported a significant rise in influenza A cases, with about 70 children admitted weekly since early October, three times the number from previous weeks. In Ho Chi Minh City, acute respiratory infections have also increased. Most patients recover within a week, but complications such as pneumonia, respiratory failure, and seizures in children can occur. Medical professionals emphasized the importance of timely treatment, using antiviral drugs under guidance, and avoiding unnecessary antibiotic use. They also recommended annual flu vaccination as a key preventive measure. The situation is being closely monitored, with concerns raised about the potential severity of the illness in vulnerable populations.
Original source text (English)
Tuesday, October 27 2026 Vietnam Law & Legal Forum Magazine Bizhub Ovietnam About Us Contact Us Việt Nam News Politics & Law Economy Sci-Tech Society Life & Style Sports Environment Opinion World E-Paper Brandinfo Sunday/Weekend Features Travel Politics & Law Society Economy Business Beat Talking Shop Environment Talk Around Town Opinion Outlook Op-Ed In the Spotlight Life & Style Expat Corner nom-nom World Sports Video Photo E-Paper Focus Bringing Resolutions to Life Viet Nam New Era Hanoi Investment Promotion Land Law Insights Hanoi Tourism Ho Chi Minh City in focus Việt Nam Insight IUU Combat 500-day campaign Happy National Day Home Society Doctors warn of serious complications from seasonal flu October 21, 2026 - 07:42 Your browser does not support the audio element. At Hà Nội Children's Hospital, flu patients currently account for about half of pediatric inpatients in its infectious disease department. Children with influenza A being cared at Thanh Nhàn Hospital in Hà Nội in mid-October. — VNA/VNS Photo Lê Đông HÀ NỘI — The number of seasonal flu cases is on the rise in several localities, particularly among young children, while doctors have warned that the illness can cause serious complications despite most patients recovering within about a week. In the northern region, cases of influenza A among children have risen in recent weeks. The National Children's Hospital has received about 70 children with influenza A each week since early October, three times the number recorded in previous weeks, Tuổi Trẻ (Youth) newspaper reported. Most of the patients were under five years old, with some developing complications including pneumonia and respiratory failure. At Hà Nội Children's Hospital, flu patients currently account for about half of pediatric inpatients in its infectious disease department. The number of children seeking medical care for flu has increased since late August, with about 100 children visiting the hospital each day, of whom 10-20 per cent require hospitalisation. In HCM City, disease surveillance has also recorded a rise in acute respiratory infections. During the first week of October, the city recorded 151 flu cases, bringing the total since the beginning of the year to 397, with influenza A/H1N1 2009 accounting for the majority. Dr Đỗ Thiện Hải, deputy director of the National Children's Hospital's Centre for Tropical Diseases, said flu is common among children and cases may increase during seasonal transitions. Children under five are particularly susceptible, partly because they often spend time in schools and daycare centres, where viruses can spread easily, Hải said. Flu and the common cold share some symptoms, but there are differences. Children with flu typically develop a sudden high fever lasting about three days, accompanied by respiratory symptoms such as coughing, sneezing and a runny nose. Common colds generally cause milder symptoms and patients tend to recover more quickly, he said. Dr Lê Văn Chương, deputy head of the Infectious Diseases Department at Thủ Đức City Hospital in HCM City, warned that seasonal flu is not simply a common cold and can cause serious complications, particularly among high-risk groups. Older people, pregnant women, children and those with chronic conditions such as cardiovascular, respiratory or kidney diseases, anemia, metabolic disorders or weakened immune systems face a higher risk of severe illness, Chương said. People with underlying respiratory or cardiovascular conditions are particularly vulnerable to adverse outcomes from flu, while those taking immunosuppressive drugs or undergoing cancer treatment may develop more severe illness and require longer treatment, Hải said. Complications can include pneumonia and respiratory failure, while high fever may cause seizures in children. Although most patients recover within about a week, people should seek medical attention if symptoms persist or become unusual. Patients with persistent high fever, poor response to fever-reducing medication, severe fatigue or poor appetite should be taken to a medical facility for evaluation and timely treatment, doctors advised. No self-medication Doctors also warned against the self-directed use of antiviral drugs and antibiotics to treat flu at home. Antiviral drugs can shorten the duration of illness and reduce the risk of complications in some cases, particularly when treatment begins early, usually within 48 hours of symptom onset, Dr Chương said. Such drugs should be used under medical guidance rather than purchased and taken without consultation, he said. Antibiotics do not kill flu viruses and therefore should not routinely be used to treat influenza. Doctors may prescribe them when medically indicated, such as when a bacterial infection occurs alongside the flu. Doctors also recommended that families take preventive measures for children and older people, with annual flu vaccination being an important way to reduce the risk of infection and severe illness. — VNS Tags close Advertisements zodiac new ranking producer (EN)-20261026-14:18 arrow_forward_ios Read more CANCEL NEXT VIDEO play_arrow volume_mute AD PausePlay % buffered00:00 00:03 00:36 UnmuteMute Play E-paper E-paper Subscribe Print Advertisement Online Advertisement Latest Most read HKSTP Partners with Leading Science and Technology Company Merck to Open a New Chapter in I&T Collaboration 1. B routine priorityPublic-health news

600 citizens are vaccinated against seasonal flu in four days, the greatest interest is among the elderly - KOHA.net

News Brief
A vaccination campaign against seasonal flu has begun in Kosovo, with 600 citizens receiving the vaccine in the first four days at the Main Family Medicine Center in Pristina and 15 other centers. The elderly and individuals with chronic diseases have shown the highest interest in getting vaccinated. The campaign was delayed by three weeks, as vaccines were scheduled to arrive by the end of September but arrived later. Seasonal influenza is an acute respiratory infection that spreads easily through droplets and can lead to serious complications, particularly in vulnerable groups such as the elderly and those with chronic illnesses. The World Health Organization (WHO) recommends annual vaccination to reduce the risk of severe flu and health complications, along with hygiene measures like regular hand washing and covering the mouth and nose when coughing or sneezing. The state provides free vaccines, and the service is described as excellent. No confirmed cases or deaths are reported in the supplied evidence.

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Seasonal influenza can cause serious complications in humans; the Ministry of Health recommends 8 preventive measures. - Vietnam.vn

News Brief
The Ministry of Health in Vietnam has recommended 8 preventive measures for seasonal influenza, warning that the disease can cause serious complications, particularly in high-risk groups such as the elderly, children, and those with underlying health conditions. The virus is primarily transmitted through respiratory droplets, and the risk of infection is high in crowded or enclosed spaces. The Department of Disease Prevention will continue to monitor the situation and provide guidance.
Original source text (English)
English Login Homepage Topic News Political System Destination Event Travel Happy Vietnam Enterprise Product Heritage Museum Figure Multimedia Data Home Culture – Society Medical Seasonal influenza can cause serious complications in humans; the Ministry of Health recommends 8 preventive measures. Seasonal influenza is primarily transmitted from person to person through respiratory droplets containing the virus, which are released when an infected person coughs, sneezes, or talks. The risk of infection is especially high with close contact with an infected person, in crowded places, or in enclosed, poorly ventilated spaces. Báo Đại biểu Nhân dân•21/10/2026 Follow Vietnam.vn on Google Add Vietnam.vn on Google 0 According to the Department of Disease Prevention, Ministry of Health , seasonal influenza is an acute infectious disease caused by the influenza virus, circulating year-round in Vietnam. The number of cases may increase at certain times of the year, depending on the region and weather conditions, especially during transitional seasons and the cold months of autumn and winter. The disease is primarily transmitted from person to person through respiratory droplets containing the virus, which are spread when an infected person coughs, sneezes, or talks. The risk of infection increases with close contact with an infected person, in crowded places, or in enclosed, poorly ventilated spaces. Most people with the flu experience mild symptoms and recover on their own. However, the disease can still become severe and cause complications, especially in high-risk groups such as people over 65, children under 5, pregnant women, women who have given birth within the last two weeks, and people with underlying health conditions. Khám phá thêm Tìm hiểu lịch sử Đọc Mẹo Du Lịch Khám phá ĐNA Underlying conditions that increase the risk of severe complications include cardiovascular disease, chronic lung disease, kidney failure, cirrhosis, diabetes, and immunodeficiency. Therefore, proactive prevention, symptom recognition, and health monitoring are crucial in limiting the risk of infection and complications. Underlying conditions that increase the risk of developing severe seasonal influenza include cardiovascular disease, chronic lung disease, kidney failure, cirrhosis, diabetes, and immunodeficiency. Seasonal influenza can cause serious complications, so the Ministry of Health recommends that people take the following 8 preventive measures: You may also like - Wear a mask correctly when visiting healthcare facilities, using public transportation, going to crowded places, or enclosed spaces with poor ventilation. - Cover your mouth and nose when coughing or sneezing with a disposable tissue or your elbow. Dispose of used tissues properly, and wash your hands immediately afterward. - Maintain personal hygiene, wash hands frequently with soap and clean water or hand sanitizer; avoid touching your eyes, nose, and mouth. - Limit close contact with people who have or are suspected of having respiratory illnesses. - Maintain a healthy lifestyle, exercise regularly, eat a balanced diet, get enough sleep, and keep your body warm to boost your immune system. - Individuals experiencing symptoms such as fever, cough, sore throat, and runny nose should limit contact with others, rest, monitor their health, and wear a mask when interacting with others. Patients should not self-medicate with antiviral or antibiotic drugs without a prescription from a healthcare professional. You may also like - Regularly clean and sanitize frequently touched surfaces; ensure living spaces, workplaces, and classrooms are well-ventilated, and improve ventilation in enclosed areas. - Individuals at high risk of developing severe symptoms from the flu should limit their exposure to crowded places. If symptoms appear, they should proactively contact or visit a healthcare facility for timely advice, examination, and treatment. At the same time, the Department of Disease Prevention will continue to coordinate with relevant units to monitor the developments of seasonal influenza, update information, provide professional guidance, and implement appropriate prevention and control measures. Location and population
Seasonal influenza is primarily transmitted from person to person through respiratory droplets containing the virus, which are released when an infected person coughs, sneezes, or talks.
Event
The Ministry of Health recommends 8 preventive measures for seasonal influenza, emphasizing that the disease can cause serious complications, especially in high-risk groups.
Scale, impact and risk
The disease is primarily transmitted through respiratory droplets and the risk of infection is high in crowded places or enclosed spaces. The Ministry recommends proactive prevention and health monitoring.
Response, status and uncertainty
The Department of Disease Prevention will continue to monitor the developments of seasonal influenza and provide professional guidance.

Original source text (English)
Early Flu Season Is Here. A Mutated H1N1 Virus May Be Driving Cases Life-Changing Amazon Finds Under $50, According To Editors IE 11 is not supported. For an optimal experience visit our site on another browser. Skip to Content On the Show About TODAY Show Video 3rd Hour of TODAY Jenna & Sheinelle Saturday TODAY Sunday TODAY Shop the Show Podcasts TODAY Plaza Citi Concert Series on TODAY From the Archives Shop Shop the Show Toy Awards Deals and Sales Product Finder Gift Guides Shop TODAY Savings Ranked by Shop TODAY Steals & Deals Stuff We Love Newsletter Add Us To Google Award Winners Step Into Style Health Start TODAY Expert Tip of the Day Women's Health Fitness Diet & Nutrition Mental Health Sleep Start TODAY Parents Baby Names Kids Health Family Babies Parenting Tips LOL Food Recipes on the Show Most Popular Recipes All Recipes Food News Life Astrology Holidays Quotes Jokes Trivia Sign up / Sign in Hi Flu Activity Is Ramping Up Early This Year. Is a Mutated H1N1 Strain to Blame? Share this — TODAY DashboardMy SavedNewslettersProfile Start TODAY Subscription Steals & Deals, Boxes & Last Chance Market Purchased Order History Sign Out Search Today Sections Health & Wellness Parenting Food Shop Pop Culture News Life Style Shows TODAY 3rd Hour of TODAY TODAY with Jenna & Sheinelle Saturday TODAY Sunday TODAY with Willie Geist Watch Full Episodes More News Life Books Trending Recipes Read With Jenna Astrology Inspirational Holidays Relationships TODAY Recipes Newsletters Start TODAY TODAY Archives Shop TODAY Awards Citi Concert Series Podcasts About Follow today More Brands Facebook Pinterest Email SMS Blue Sky Print Whatsapp Reddit Flipboard Twitter Linkedin On the Show Shop Health Start TODAY Parents Food Life Flu Activity Is Ramping Up Early This Year. Is a Mutated H1N1 Strain to Blame? Flu activity is surging sooner than usual as an influenza A H1N1 strain with a new mutation spreads across the U.S. Here’s what to know. Updated Oct. 26, 2026, 4:50 PM UTC / Source: TODAY Caroline Kee Health reporter 0 seconds of 2 minutes, 59 secondsVolume 0% Press shift question mark to access a list of keyboard shortcuts Keyboard ShortcutsEnabledDisabled Play/PauseSPACE Increase Volume↑ Decrease Volume↓ Seek Forward→ Seek Backward← Captions On/Offc Fullscreen/Exit Fullscreenf Mute/Unmutem Decrease Caption Size- Increase Caption Size+ or = Seek %0-9 Settings OffCCEnglish Font Color White Font Opacity 100% Font Size 100% Font Family Arial Character Edge None Background Color Black Background Opacity 50% Window Color Black Window Opacity 0% Reset WhiteBlackRedGreenBlueYellowMagentaCyan 100%75%50%25% 200%175%150%125%100%75%50% ArialCourierGeorgiaImpactLucida ConsoleTahomaTimes New RomanTrebuchet MSVerdana NoneRaisedDepressedUniformDrop Shadow WhiteBlackRedGreenBlueYellowMagentaCyan 100%75%50%25%0% WhiteBlackRedGreenBlueYellowMagentaCyan 100%75%50%25%0% Auto540p (1819 kbps)1080p (5000 kbps)720p (3609 kbps)540p (1819 kbps)360p (974 kbps)270p (512 kbps)270p (286 kbps) Live 00:02 02:57 02:59 Flu Season 2026 Arrives Early: What You Need to Know 02:59 Share Savewith a NBCUniversal Profile Add TODAY to Google If you’re surprised to hear about people getting sick with the flu before Halloween, you’re not alone. The 2026-2027 flu season appears to be off to an early and strong start, experts say. Influenza activity is still low nationally, but cases are rising quickly in many parts of the country, especially the West, and two states, Louisiana and Hawaii, are already reporting moderate levels. As of Oct. 21, flu infections are growing or likely growing in 44 states, according to the latest estimates from the U.S. Centers for Disease Control and Prevention. That’s not surprising, given that flu activity typically ticks up around this time of year. However, this year’s flu season started way ahead of schedule, with infections rising as early as August. “Early-season influenza activity is currently higher than typically observed at this time of year,” the CDC said in its FluView surveillance report for the week ending Oct. 17. Influenza A H1N1 is fueling the vast majority of cases in the U.S. so far this season, per CDC data. That’s a shift from last winter, when H3N2 subclade K — aka, the “superflu” — was the dominant strain. What’s more, it appears that the H1N1 strain circulating has picked up a new mutation which may affect its ability to evade pre-existing immunity. If H1N1 sounds familiar, that’s because it’s the infamous “swine flu” strain that caused a pandemic in 2009. As the mutated H1N1 strain spreads, doctors are urging people to get the 2026-2027 flu vaccine. Why Is Flu Season Starting So Early? No two flu seasons look the same because the timing and severity can vary from year to year. It’s still too early to tell if this flu season will end up being more severe or what role the mutated H1N1 strain might play, the CDC tells TODAY.com in a statement. However, there are multiple indicators that flu activity is higher than usual for this time of year. Cases started increasing in late August and early September and have been rising steadily since, per the CDC. The percent of flu tests coming back positive was 5.7% during the week ending Oct. 17. Last year during the same time period, the weekly test positivity rate was less than 1%, per the CDC National Respiratory and Enteric Virus Surveillance System dashboard. Outpatient visits to doctor’s offices or urgent cares for flu-like illness hit 2.2% during the week ending Oct. 17, which is higher than the previous two seasons, CDC data show. Hospitalizations and deaths from the flu are also rising. “Cases are still relatively low, so we’re still in the early phases of a flu surge,” Andrew Pekosz, Ph.D., a virologist at the Johns Hopkins Bloomberg School of Public Health, tells TODAY.com. It’s unclear why exactly cases are rising earlier than normal. One potential reason is that people may have less pre-existing immunity to the H1N1 strain with a new mutation. “Maybe that’s why people are getting sick sooner,” Dr. Bernard Camins, an infectious disease physician and professor at the Icahn School of Medicine at Mount Sinai, tells TODAY.com. The H1N1 strain spreading is different from the H3N2 strain that dominated last winter. “Even people who got the flu last year are vulnerable,” says Camins. When H1N1 started ramping up in late August, the updated flu shots had just started rolling out. “The pattern we oftentimes see with early season flu is that the first strain that gets here is the one that escapes immunity the most,” Pekosz adds. Does an early flu season mean a more severe flu season? Not necessarily, experts say. It’s too early to tell how the 2026-2027 flu season will pan out, or whether it’ll be milder compared to last year’s. The U.S. saw a harsh flu season last winter fueled by the highly mutated H3N2 subclade K variant, aka the “super flu.” “We still have to see how bad it will be this year, but I think it is just starting earlier,” says Camins. “The only thing predictable about the flu is that it’s unpredictable,” Dr. Natalie Azar, NBC News medical contributor, said on TODAY. What Is the Mutated H1N1 Strain? Influenza viruses mutate constantly, which is why we get an updated flu shot every year. Last summer, H3N2 picked up a host of mutations, which led to the emergence the subclade K variant. H1N1 viruses typically change less often than H3N2 viruses, the CDC says. However, this year, H1N1 (pdm09), subclade D.3.1.1, acquired one new mutation, G155E, says Pekosz. Recommended Gift Guides Gift GuidesHold Off the Socks — We Have Editor-Picked Gifts for Young Men Gift Guides Gift GuidesThe Best and Most Useful Gifts for 21-Year-Olds The mutation was first identified in September in Seattle, Washington, according in a preprint paper published to bioRxiv. “It is different from last year’s H1N1 virus, and that mutation position (G155E) we know is important for generating protective antibody responses,” Pekosz explains. This means the H1N1 strain may be better able to escape immunity because the antibodies people have from previous infection or vaccination may not recognize it as well. “How that (mutation) is going to affect the disease spread and severity is not clear,” says Pekosz. Even if the H1N1 strain circulating does not match the one included in the 2026-2027 flu vaccines, the shot is still expected to provide protection. “A mismatch is not a black-or-white situation. We may have less immunity, not no immunity,” Pekosz adds. Historically, H1N1 has hit young people particularly hard. The new mutation may make children more susceptible, especially if they haven’t been exposed to H1N1 yet, Azar said. “It is too early to know what viruses may predominate or to say whether there might be implications on flu vaccine effectiveness this season,” the CDC said in its latest FluView report. H1N1 Flu Symptoms The flu often hits suddenly and intensely, says Camins. Common symptoms of the flu include: Fever Chills Fatigue Body aches Runny nose Coughing It can be difficult to distinguish the signs of a flu infection from those caused by COVID, which is why testing is important, Camins says. If you test positive for influenza, you can seek treatment with antivirals, which can lessen symptom severity and duration. Antivirals are an especially important tool for high-risk groups, including people over the age of 65, children under the age of 5, and immunocompromised individuals. These medications aren’t available over-the-counter, so you’ll need to see a doctor first. “You have to get that prescription fast ... within the first 48 hours after symptom onset, (or else) they don’t work as well,” says Pekosz. If you experience severe flu symptoms, contact your doctor. When to Get the Flu Shot There’s no reason to panic over the mutated H1N1 strain, the experts emphasize, but it is a good reminder to protect yourself. “View (this) as a a sign to go out and get the flu vaccine,” says Pekosz. Getting a flu shot is the most effective way to prevent against severe illness, complications and death from the flu. The 2026-2027 flu vaccines are available at most pharmacies, doctors’ offices and clinics. The updated shots protect against three viruses: influenza A H1N1 (pdm09), influenza A H3N2 and influenza B. There are multiple options available, including standard flu shots, high-dose shots, a nasal mist and the new mRNA flu vaccine. “Even if there’s a mismatched strain, there’s two other strains that the vaccine will provide better protection against,” says Pekosz. Different flu strains can circulate later in the season, so the shot can protect you throughout the winter and early spring. The flu shot is never 100% effective, Camins adds, and you can still get infected if you’ve been vaccinated — but you won’t get nearly as sick or end up in the hospital. Vaccination is especially important if you’re considered high-risk, but everyone can benefit. The best time to get the flu shot is before the end of October, but you can still benefit if you get it later, the experts note. “If you haven’t gotten it yet for whatever reason, now’s the time to get vaccinated because the flu season is starting a little bit sooner and you need at least two weeks for the vaccine immunity to (reach) protective levels,” Pekosz adds. If you aren’t sure which flu vaccine you should get, talk to your doctor or pharmacist. B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

2026 年流感季节结束 - NICD

End of the 2026 influenza season - NICD
新闻简报南非2026年流感季节于第39周结束,于2026年9月27日结束。该季节于2026年3月9日开始的第11周开始,当时公立医院住院肺炎监测患者的流感检出率超过季节性阈值,并在两周内保持较高水平。流感活动在2026年5月11日开始的第20周达到顶峰。从2025年12月29日到2026年10月11日,在三个监测系统中,检测的6212份样本中有1029份检测结果呈流感阳性,占所有检测的16.6%。其中,甲型H3N2流感病毒 占 44.0%,甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 占 32.6%,乙型流感占 20.5%。还发现了 7 例双重感染。从2026年9月28日开始的第40周,检出率跌破季节性阈值,并连续两周保持低位。建议临床医生继续在 诊断 呼吸道疾病和具有高度提示性症状的患者中考虑流感,即使在季节正式结束后也是如此。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年流感季节于第 39 周(截至 2026 年 9 月 27 日的一周)结束。
地点与对象
在南非,流感季节通常在四月中下旬左右开始,在八月下旬至九月中旬左右结束。2026年流感季从第11周(2026年3月9日开始的一周)开始,公立医院入院肺炎住院患者监测的流感检出率超过季节性阈值,并连续两周保持在该阈值以上。
事件
这比之前的大多数赛季要早,但与 2025 年相似,当时赛季于第 13 周(从 2025 年 3 月 24 日开始的一周)开始。
规模、影响与风险
从2025年12月29日到2026年10月11日,在三个哨兵监测系统中,在6212份测试样本中的1029份检测到流感(16.6%)。在可确定亚型的病例中(97.1%;999/1029),44.0%(453/1029)为甲型H3N2流感病毒流感,32.6%(335/1029)为甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09流感,20.5%(211/1029)为B型流感。此外,还发现7例双重感染。
应对、状态与不确定性
流感活动随后增加并在第 20 周(自 2026 年 5 月 11 日开始的一周)达到顶峰。随后,检测率从第 40 周(2026 年 9 月 28 日开始的一周)开始降至季节性阈值以下,并连续两周保持在阈值以下。临床医生应继续在出现呼吸道疾病的患者和具有高度提示性症状的患者的 诊断 鉴别诊断中考虑流感。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

End of the 2026 influenza season - NICD

News Brief
The 2026 influenza season in South Africa concluded in week 39, ending on 27 September 2026. The season began in week 11, starting on 9 March 2026, when the influenza detection rate among inpatient pneumonia surveillance patients at public hospitals surpassed the seasonal threshold and remained elevated for two weeks. Influenza activity peaked in week 20, which started on 11 May 2026. From 29 December 2025 to 11 October 2026, across three surveillance systems, 1029 out of 6212 specimens tested were positive for influenza, representing 16.6% of all tests. Among these, 44.0% were A(H3N2), 32.6% were A(H1N1)pdm09, and 20.5% were influenza B. Seven dual infections were also identified. The detection rate fell below the seasonal threshold in week 40, starting on 28 September 2026, and remained low for two consecutive weeks. Clinicians are advised to continue considering influenza in the differential diagnosis for respiratory illnesses and patients with highly suggestive symptoms, even after the season has officially ended.
Original source text (English)

Newsletter Videos Podcasts Contact Us Our Office General Enquiries Media Enquiries NMC Enquiries Disease Index Search Close End of the 2026 influenza season 21 October , 2026 The 2026 influenza season ended in week 39 (week ending 27 September 2026). In South Africa, the influenza season typically begins around mid-to-late April and ends around late August to mid-September. The 2026 season began in week 11 (week starting 9 March 2026), when the influenza detection rate among patients enrolled in inpatient pneumonia surveillance at public hospitals exceeded the seasonal threshold and remained above it for two consecutive weeks. This was earlier than most previous seasons but similar to 2025, when the season started in week 13 (week starting 24 March 2025). Influenza activity subsequently increased and peaked in week 20 (week starting 11 May 2026). The detection rate subsequently dropped below the seasonal threshold from week 40 (week starting 28 September 2026) and remained below the threshold for two consecutive weeks. From 29 December 2025 to 11 October 2026 across the three sentinel surveillance systems (outpatient influenza-like-illness (ILI) surveillance at public primary health care clinics, outpatient ILI surveillance at private general practitioner practices, and inpatient pneumonia surveillance at public hospitals), influenza was detected in 1029 of 6212 specimens tested (16.6%) . Among the cases for which a subtype could be determined (97.1%; 999/1029), 44.0% (453/1029) were influenza A(H3N2), 32.6% (335/1029) were influenza A(H1N1)pdm09 and 20.5% (211/1029) were influenza B (211/1029; 20.5%). Additionally, seven dual infections were detected: one case of A(H1N1) and A(H3N2), one case of A(H3N2) and influenza B, two cases of A(H1N1) and influenza B, and three cases of an inconclusive influenza A subtype and influenza B. Before routine lineage subtyping of influenza B was discontinued in June 2026, 100% (111/111) of tested influenza B samples were B/Victoria. Message to clinicians Influenza is an acute respiratory infection caused by influenza viruses. It spreads easily between people through respiratory droplets and causes seasonal epidemics. Most people recover without treatment, but influenza can cause severe illness or death, especially in people at high risk of severe influenza illness or complications. Although the 2026 influenza season has ended, sporadic influenza infections may continue to occur outside the defined seasonal period. Clinicians should therefore continue to consider influenza in the differential diagnosis of patients presenting with respiratory illness and in patients with highly suggestive symptoms. High-risk groups include pregnant women, individuals living with HIV, those with chronic conditions such as diabetes, lung disease, tuberculosis, heart disease, renal disease, and obesity, older individuals (≥65 years), and children under two years old. Where to find updated data Weekly reports documenting respiratory pathogen circulation in South Africa, including influenza, respiratory syncytial virus (RSV), severe acute respiratory syndrome coronavirus 2 (SARS-CoV-2), Bordetella pertussis, human adenovirus, human metapneumovirus, human parainfluenza virus and human rhinovirus, are available on the NICD website here. View five news elements
Time
The 2026 influenza season ended in week 39 (week ending 27 September 2026).
Location and population
In South Africa, the influenza season typically begins around mid-to-late April and ends around late August to mid-September. The 2026 season began in week 11 (week starting 9 March 2026), when the influenza detection rate among patients enrolled in inpatient pneumonia surveillance at public hospitals exceeded the seasonal threshold and remained above it for two consecutive weeks.
Event
This was earlier than most previous seasons but similar to 2025, when the season started in week 13 (week starting 24 March 2025).
Scale, impact and risk
From 29 December 2025 to 11 October 2026 across the three sentinel surveillance systems, influenza was detected in 1029 of 6212 specimens tested (16.6%). Among the cases for which a subtype could be determined (97.1%; 999/1029), 44.0% (453/1029) were influenza A(H3N2), 32.6% (335/1029) were influenza A(H1N1)pdm09, and 20.5% (211/1029) were influenza B. Additionally, seven dual infections were detected.
Response, status and uncertainty
Influenza activity subsequently increased and peaked in week 20 (week starting 11 May 2026). The detection rate subsequently dropped below the seasonal threshold from week 40 (week starting 28 September 2026) and remained below the threshold for two consecutive weeks. Clinicians should continue to consider influenza in the differential diagnosis of patients presenting with respiratory illness and in patients with highly suggestive symptoms.

变异 H1N1 引发 疫苗 担忧,美国早期流感季节激增 - International Business Times UK

Early US Flu Season Surges as Mutated H1N1 Raises Vaccine Concerns - International Business Times UK
新闻简报美国流感季节初期,流感活动激增,西海岸报告的流感活动在 2026 年 10 月上旬达到最高水平。美国疾病控制与预防中心 (CDC) 指出,虽然 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 菌株在全国病例中占主导地位,但没有证据表明 G155E 突变 会导致更严重的疾病或流感活动的早期上升。截至 10 月 17 日的一周,5.7% 的呼吸道标本检测呈流感阳性,其中 97.9% 为甲型流感,其中 99.2% 的 A 病毒被鉴定为 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09。疾病预防控制中心强调,全国流感水平仍然较低,提前开始并不一定意味着流感季节会更加严重。他们建议 6 个月及以上的所有人(尤其是高危人群)每年进行一次 疫苗接种,以降低严重并发症、住院和死亡的风险。尽管社交媒体上有一些公开辩论质疑 疫苗 的有效性,但 CDC 仍坚持其对 疫苗 和标准预防措施的立场。
查看新闻五要素
时间
美国流感活动的早期上升始于 2026 年 8 月下旬和 9 月,并在 10 月 10 日结束的一周达到最高活动记录。CDC 的调查结果于 2026 年 10 月 24 日之前报告。
地点与对象
西海岸,包括 HHS 第 9 区和第 10 区,是流感活动最严重的地区。受影响人群包括六个月及以上的个人,特别是幼儿、65 岁及以上的成年人、孕妇和有潜在健康问题的人等高危人群。
事件
美国的流感活动比平常更早上升,其中 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 菌株在全国病例中占主导地位。一些 H1N1 病毒中已鉴定出 G155E 突变,这可能会影响抗体识别病毒的方式。然而,CDC尚未证实该突变会导致更严重的疾病或活动的早期激增。
规模、影响与风险
截至 2026 年 10 月 17 日的一周,5.7% 的呼吸道样本检测结果呈流感阳性,其中 97.9% 为甲型流感。在甲型流感病例中,99.2% 为 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09。CDC 指出,全国总体水平仍然较低,并且没有证据表明 G155E 突变 会增加严重程度或导致活动提前增加。CDC 建议每年进行 疫苗接种,以降低严重并发症、住院和死亡的风险,特别是对于高危人群。
应对、状态与不确定性
CDC 正在监测情况,尚未确定 G155E 突变 对 疫苗 有效性的影响。抗原测试发现,雪貂抗 疫苗 参考病毒的抗血清可以很好地识别大多数 H1N1 病毒,但这并不能证实 疫苗 真实世界的有效性。CDC 继续建议年度 疫苗接种 和标准预防措施,例如洗手和生病时呆在家里。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Early US Flu Season Surges as Mutated H1N1 Raises Vaccine Concerns - International Business Times UK

News Brief
The early US flu season has seen a surge in activity, with the West Coast reporting the highest levels by early October 2026. The Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC) noted that while the A(H1N1)pdm09 strain dominates national cases, there is no evidence that the G155E mutation causes more severe illness or the early rise in flu activity. For the week ending 17 October, 5.7% of respiratory specimens tested were positive for influenza, with 97.9% being influenza A, and 99.2% of these A viruses identified as A(H1N1)pdm09. The CDC emphasizes that national flu levels remain low and that the early start does not necessarily indicate a more severe season. They recommend annual vaccination for everyone aged six months and older, especially high-risk groups, to reduce the risk of serious complications, hospitalization, and death. Despite some public debate on social media questioning vaccine effectiveness, the CDC maintains its stance on vaccination and standard prevention measures.

INTERNATIONAL BUSINESS TIMES uk Newsletter My account Log Out Oct 26, 11:04 PM GMT World UK News Markets Business SME Personal Finance Tech Entertainment Lifestyle Reviews Newsletter My account Log Out UK Edition World UK News Markets Business - Retail - Hospitality - Healthcare - Electrification of Auto - Finance & Banking - Cybersecurity - Artificial Intelligence - New Cities - Education - esport - Healthcare SME Personal Finance Tech Entertainment Lifestyle Reviews Listings & More Newsletter Fintech Shopping and Sponsored Latest News Most Popular News Trending Sports Industry Reports Editions Australia Edition India Edition Singapore Edition United Kingdom United States Newsletter Follow Us Editions Australia Edition India Edition Singapore Edition United Kingdom United States World Early US Flu Season Surges as Mutated H1N1 Raises Vaccine Concerns The West Coast has the most activity, but CDC says national levels are still low and there's no evidence that G155E causes more severe illness By Ma. Patrice Anne A. Martin Published 24 October 2026, 6:50 AM BST Share on Facebook Share on Twitter Share on LinkedIn Share on Reddit Share on Flipboard Share on Pocket US flu activity rises early as mutated H1N1 raises questions about vaccine protection Polina Tankilevitch/Pexels Flu activity in the US is climbing earlier than usual, with the West Coast already seeing the highest levels by early October and the A(H1)pdm09 strain dominating national cases. Scientists are closely tracking an emerging G155E mutation in some H1N1 viruses that affects an antigenic region of the virus and may alter how antibodies recognise it, although overall activity across the country remains low. US flu activity began increasing in late August and September 2026, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). By the week ending 10 October, the West Coast, including HHS regions 9 and 10, was recording the highest activity. For the week ending 17 October, 2,918 of 51,490 respiratory specimens tested by clinical laboratories were positive for influenza, giving a positivity rate of 5.7 per cent. Influenza A accounted for 97.9 per cent of positive specimens. Although activity is increasing, national levels remain low overall. An early start does not necessarily mean a more severe flu season. H1N1 Dominates Early Flu Activity as G155E Spreads The CDC reported that 239 of 241 influenza A viruses subtyped by public health laboratories during the week ending 17 October were A(H1N1)pdm09, representing 99.2 per cent of those analysed. A(H1N1)pdm09 is an established influenza virus that emerged in 2009 and continues to circulate seasonally. Most A(H1N1)pdm09 viruses genetically characterised by the CDC this season belong to the D.3.1.1 subclade. Within this group, viruses carrying the G155E amino-acid substitution in the haemagglutinin protein have increased in recent weeks. The mutation occurs in an antigenic region of the virus and may affect how antibodies recognise some circulating viruses. The World Health Organization (WHO) reported in October that viruses carrying G155E had increased in recent weeks. Human-serum studies cited by WHO found reduced antibody responses to some recently circulating D.3.1.1 viruses carrying G155E and other substitutions, raising questions about how effectively existing antibodies recognise some circulating variants. However, the CDC's separate antigenic testing found that 82 of 88 characterised H1N1 viruses were well recognised by ferret antisera to the vaccine reference virus. This laboratory finding does not establish how effectively vaccination protects people against circulating variants in real-world conditions. The CDC says it is too early to determine the impact of these genetic changes on vaccine effectiveness. There is also insufficient evidence that G155E caused the early rise in flu activity or makes the virus more severe. Read more What Is Shamonda Virus? Cause, Symptoms and Vaccine Questions After First Human Case in France Ends in Death WHO Chief Confirms First Human Shamonda Virus Infection After Patient Dies in France Shamonda Virus Found in Human for First Time After Decades of Being Considered Non-Transmissible PA Measles Outbreak Hits 1,286 Cases; 66 Pregnant Women Infected, at Least Six Babies Born With the Virus Experts Call For Vaccination as Monitoring Continues The CDC recommends annual flu vaccination for everyone aged six months and older, unless they have a relevant contraindication. Vaccination can reduce the risk of serious complications, hospitalisation and death, even though it does not prevent every infection. Young children, adults aged 65 and older, pregnant people and those with certain underlying health conditions face a higher risk of severe flu complications. The CDC also recommends handwashing, covering coughs and sneezes, and staying home when sick. People at higher risk who develop flu symptoms should seek medical advice promptly, as antiviral treatment can be most beneficial when started early. Early Flu Surge Sparks Debate Over Vaccination on Social Media The early rise in flu activity has prompted debate on social media, with users sharing personal experiences and questioning the effectiveness of flu vaccines. One user questioned the argument that vaccination may have reduced the severity of illness, pointing to cases in which vaccinated people still contracted flu. My favorite part of flu season is when the same people who got the flu shot get the flu and say well imagine how bad it would have been without it. — David Wolfe (@DavidWolfe) October 23, 2026 Another user claimed to have gone a decade without receiving a flu shot or contracting flu. This year will make a decade since I have received a flu shot and it will also be a decade since I have contracted the flu. It’s funny how that works. — Steve 🇺🇸 (@SteveLovesAmmo) October 23, 2026 Other users pushed back, with one expressing frustration over deaths among unvaccinated people, while another challenged the suggestion that fever or illness after vaccination proves that vaccines are ineffective. My least favourite part of flu season is when I see unvaccinated people die from flu https://t.co/92f6vTccye — Neil Stone (@DrNeilStone) October 23, 2026 As surveillance continues, researchers will need further data to determine whether G155E affects protection against circulating strains. The CDC continues to recommend annual vaccination to reduce the risk of serious flu complications. Time
The early rise in US flu activity began in late August and September 2026, with the highest activity recorded by the week ending 10 October. The CDC's findings were reported by 24 October 2026.
Location and population
The West Coast, including HHS regions 9 and 10, was the location with the highest flu activity. The affected population includes individuals aged six months and older, with specific emphasis on high-risk groups such as young children, adults aged 65 and older, pregnant people, and those with underlying health conditions.
Event
Flu activity in the US is rising earlier than usual, with the A(H1N1)pdm09 strain dominating national cases. A G155E mutation in some H1N1 viruses has been identified, which may affect how antibodies recognize the virus. However, the CDC has not confirmed that this mutation causes more severe illness or the early surge in activity.
Scale, impact and risk
For the week ending 17 October 2026, 5.7% of respiratory specimens tested were positive for influenza, with 97.9% of these being influenza A. Among influenza A cases, 99.2% were A(H1N1)pdm09. The CDC notes that national levels remain low overall, and there is no evidence that the G155E mutation increases severity or causes the early rise in activity. The CDC recommends annual vaccination to reduce the risk of serious complications, hospitalization, and death, particularly for high-risk groups.

The CDC is monitoring the situation and has not yet determined the impact of the G155E mutation on vaccine effectiveness. Antigenic testing found that most H1N1 viruses are well recognized by ferret antisera to the vaccine reference virus, but this does not confirm real-world vaccine effectiveness. The CDC continues to recommend annual vaccination and standard preventive measures such as handwashing and staying home when sick.

Surprise Mutation Driving Early Start to Flu Season - Everyday Health
新闻简报甲型 H1N1 流感病毒中的一种新 突变 G155E 被怀疑是导致 2026 年流感季节提前开始的原因。这种 突变 早在 2026 年 5 月就在日本发现,它似乎会降低抗体识别该病毒的能力,可能导致 传播 增加。早期数据显示,流感活动显着上升,检测阳性率从 9 月初的 1.3% 攀升至 10 月中旬的近 6%。自一个月前以来,流感住院人数增加了一倍多,达到 4,800 多例。然而,目前还没有证据表明 突变 会导致更严重的疾病。专家们正在密切关注局势,并指出早期激增可能是由于免疫逃避而不是“超级流感”。CDC 将总体流感活动归类为“低”,但 突变 对公共卫生的影响仍在调查中。

B routine priorityPublic-health news

Surprise Mutation Driving Early Start to Flu Season - Everyday Health

News Brief
A new mutation in the influenza A(H1N1) virus, G155E, is suspected to be driving the early start of the flu season in 2026. This mutation, identified in Japan as early as May 2026, appears to reduce the ability of antibodies to recognize the virus, potentially leading to increased transmission. Early data shows a significant rise in flu activity, with test positivity rates climbing from 1.3% in early September to nearly 6% in mid-October. Flu hospitalizations have more than doubled since a month ago, reaching over 4,800 cases. However, there is no evidence yet that the mutation is causing more severe illness. Experts are monitoring the situation closely, noting that the early surge may be due to immune evasion rather than a 'super flu.' The CDC classifies overall flu activity as 'low,' but the mutation's impact on public health remains under investigation.

By Don Rauf Published on October 23, 2026 Fact-Checked by Emily Kay Votruba Add us on Google Flu season has arrived early this year, with a new variant of the virus already sickening thousands and sending many to the hospital. Usually in the United States, flu season begins in October, but this year cases began to rise starting in late August, according to the Centers for Disease Control and Prevention (CDC). [1] [2] Early data from the Walgreens 2026–27 Flu Index shows national flu activity tracking about four weeks ahead of expectations. [3] “By late September, test positivity was about eight times higher than at the same point last year,” says Ryan Maves, MD, a professor specializing in infectious disease at Wake Forest School of Medicine in Winston-Salem, North Carolina. Flu Numbers Are Up, and in Unexpected Places Although the CDC currently classifies overall flu activity as “low,” the national percentage of positive tests for influenza has climbed from 1.3 percent in early September to nearly 6 percent in mid-October. [1] The latest CDC surveillance data show that flu hospitalizations have more than doubled since a month ago, reaching more than 4,800 cases. “In addition to flu’s early arrival, the West Coast states are being hit first unexpectedly,” says Peter Chin-Hong, MD, an infectious-disease specialist and a professor of medicine at the University of California San Francisco School of Medicine. “Usually we see flu start off in the southern states, then move northeast, then westward across the country.” What Flu Strain Is Dominating Cases This Year? One of the forces driving the early surge in cases appears to be a new mutation in the predominant type of flu, influenza A (H1N1), which accounts for more than 96 percent of subtyped samples. [4] [5] Scientists have identified a genetic mutation, G155E, on the surface of the H1N1 protein that makes it harder for antibodies to recognize and block the virus from infecting human cells, according to Dr. Chin-Hong. “This change is making people who might have immunity carried over from last season, or vaccine immunity, not recognize the mutated flu quite as well,” he says. “It is likely more of an immune evasion advantage rather than a ‘super flu’ [associated with more severe symptoms]. There is no evidence so far that people are getting more seriously ill than usual,” adds Chin-Hong. A Mutation Detected Early in Japan May Explain Flu Rise in the West Several news sources have reported that G155E is likely causing a severe, early spike in flu in Japan. The mutation was identified there as early as May 2026, according to Northeastern Global News. [6] [7] To some degree, the flu wave in Japan connected to G155E may explain the increase in cases in the western United States, according to Thomas Russo, MD, an infectious-diseases expert at the Jacobs School of Medicine and Biomedical Sciences at SUNY Buffalo in New York. “Geographically, moving eastward from Japan, the western U.S. is the first stop,” says Dr. Russo. “In this era of international travel, this may in part explain the early rise in cases in the West.” California’s Department of Public Health is currently reporting “high” flu activity across most of the state, with the latest data indicating test positivity at nearly 10 percent. [8] In Hawaii, test positivity has reached a staggering 25.7 percent as of October 17. [9] Russo points out, however, that flu is gaining ground throughout the continental United States. “Here in Buffalo, we’re seeing more patients testing positive and landing in the hospital.” This Flu Season May Be Rough for Younger People Groups most at risk of severe illness due to flu, according to the CDC, include: [10] Adults age 65 and older Children younger than 2 years old People with weakened immune systems People who’ve had a stroke People with asthma, chronic lung disease, neurological conditions, heart disease, kidney disorders, liver disorders, and blood disorders People with severe obesity, or a body mass index of 40 or above Russo notes, however, that younger people may have less natural flu immunity and lower antibody neutralization against the circulating mutated H1N1 strain than many older adults. “In some tested serum [blood] samples from different demographic age groups, the ability for teens and people in their twenties to neutralize the virus was significantly less than in older people,” he says. “This makes sense because older people have seen a lot more influenza, so they’re more likely to develop prior antibodies that have some cross-protection, even though it’s a new variant.” Experts Say Get Your Flu Shot Now Based on past flu seasons and the latest virus trajectory, we can expect much more illness and hospitalizations in the weeks ahead, Russo says. “Flu season is well short of peak,” he says. Holiday gatherings will likely contribute to the spread, as people come together in closed spaces and air travel increases. With these factors in mind, public health authorities are urging the public to take measures now to protect themselves and others. The CDC advises getting a flu shot as soon as possible, as the annual seasonal flu vaccine is “the best way to help reduce the risk of getting flu and any of its potentially serious complications.” [11] Although the 2026–2027 flu shots were formulated before the G155E mutation emerged, the CDC still expects them to provide significant protection against severe illness this year. Research shows that during the 2022–23 flu season, many vaccinated people still got the flu, but the shot prevented about 70,000 hospitalizations. [12] In addition to vaccination, people may want to consider wearing a mask in crowded indoor environments, washing hands more often, and steering clear of others who are sick with flu, adds Dr. Maves. How Bad Will Flu Symptoms Be This Year? While flu season may have come early, there's no evidence so far that the illness itself will be more severe or that there will be any unique symptoms, according to Chin-Hong. No matter how harsh the virus proves to be, he urges the public to never underestimate the flu. “Every year influenza kills tens of thousands of Americans,” he says. “This is a virus that can cause serious pneumonia, worsening of heart and lung disease, brain disease in rare cases, and lead to hospitalizations and deaths, especially in vulnerable populations.” Most people get over the flu by taking care of themselves at home, but when physical signs become serious, you need to get immediate professional medical help. Chin-Hong says that severe symptoms that may require hospitalization include: Shortness of breath or trouble breathing Confusion Inability to keep down fluids For infants and very young children: Bluish lips or face, increased fussiness, and not urinating (fewer wet diapers) EDITORIAL SOURCES Everyday Health follows strict sourcing guidelines to ensure the accuracy of its content, outlined in our editorial policy. We use only trustworthy sources, including peer-reviewed studies, board-certified medical experts, patients with lived experience, and information from top institutions. Time
Flu season has arrived early this year, with cases beginning to rise in late August and continuing into mid-October 2026. The mutation was identified in Japan as early as May 2026.
Location and population
The United States and Japan. The mutation was detected in Japan and is now affecting the western U.S. The affected population includes people of all age groups, with particular concern for teens and young adults.
Event
A new mutation in the influenza A(H1N1) virus, known as G155E, has been identified as a factor in the early start of the flu season. This mutation may be reducing immune recognition and contributing to increased transmission and cases.
Scale, impact and risk
Early data shows a significant increase in flu activity, with national test positivity rates rising from 1.3% in early September to nearly 6% in mid-October. Flu hospitalizations have more than doubled since a month ago, reaching over 4,800 cases. There is no evidence so far that people are getting more seriously ill than usual.
Response, status and uncertainty
The CDC classifies overall flu activity as 'low,' but the early rise in cases and the presence of the G155E mutation are under investigation. Scientists are analyzing the mutation's impact on immune evasion, and experts are monitoring the situation closely.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

流感 疫苗接种 与老年人的严重后果减少和儿童的急诊就诊次数减少有关 - Pharmacy Times

Flu Vaccination Linked to Fewer Severe Outcomes in Older Adults, Fewer ED Visits in Children - Pharmacy Times
新闻简报在华盛顿特区 IDWeek 2026 上发表的两项研究提供了 季节性流感 疫苗 有效性的真实证据。第一项研究由 Geisinger Health System 进行,发现与未接种疫苗的患者相比,接受 疫苗 的老年人入住 ICU 的情况较少。由 CDC VISION Network 领导的第二项研究估计,2025-2026 年 疫苗 将儿童和青少年中与流感相关的急诊室 (ED) 和紧急护理 (UC) 遭遇的风险降低了 36%。这些发现基于回顾性 队列研究 和测试阴性设计分析,后者根据年龄、性别、种族和其他因素进行调整。Geisinger 研究纳入了 2024 年 10 月 1 日至 2025 年 4 月 30 日之间经 RT-PCR 确诊流感的 65 岁或以上成年人,而 VISION 研究涵盖了 2025 年 10 月 1 日至 2026 年 4 月 30 日期间因急性呼吸道疾病而出现 ED 或 UC 的 6 个月至 17 岁的儿童和青少年。VISION 作者强调,即使是中度流感,疫苗 的有效性可显着减轻儿童的流感负担,在 5 岁以下儿童中观察到的保护作用最高。Geisinger 团队强调需要进一步研究农村人口中 疫苗接种 的障碍,并计划使用倾向得分匹配来完善他们的分析。
查看新闻五要素
时间
在华盛顿特区举办的 IDWeek 2026 上发表的两项研究分析了 2024 年 10 月 1 日至 2025 年 4 月 30 日期间的老年人数据以及 2025 年 10 月 1 日至 2026 年 4 月 30 日期间的儿童和青少年数据。
地点与对象
Geisinger Health System 为大多数患有严重合并症的农村老年人口提供服务,并在美国设有六个 VISION 站点进行儿科分析。
事件
IDWeek 2026 上发表的两项研究发现,季节性流感 疫苗接种 与老年人较少的严重后果相关,并降低儿童和青少年患流感相关 ED 和 UC 的风险。这些发现基于回顾性 队列研究 和测试阴性设计分析。
规模、影响与风险
在老年人中,17.7%接受了疫苗接种,其中疫苗接种者中有2.5%被收入重症监护病房(ICU),而未接种疫苗者中有10.5%被收入ICU。在儿童中,22%的病例是接种过疫苗的,总体疫苗有效性(VE)估计为36%(95%置信区间,33%-39%),针对与流感相关的急诊科(ED)和住院病房(UC)就诊。
应对、状态与不确定性
这些分析未经调整,VISION 作者指出,他们的发现并不一定代表 CDC 的官方立场。盖辛格调查人员建议进一步研究农村准入和保险相关障碍,并计划采取的下一步措施包括倾向得分匹配和跨多个季节的分析。
B routine priorityPublic-health news

Flu Vaccination Linked to Fewer Severe Outcomes in Older Adults, Fewer ED Visits in Children - Pharmacy Times

News Brief
Two studies presented at IDWeek 2026 in Washington, DC, provide real-world evidence on the effectiveness of seasonal influenza vaccines. The first study, conducted by Geisinger Health System, found that older adults who received the vaccine had fewer ICU admissions compared to unvaccinated patients. The second study, led by the CDC’s VISION Network, estimated that the 2025-2026 vaccines reduced the risk of influenza-associated emergency department (ED) and urgent care (UC) encounters among children and adolescents by 36%. These findings are based on retrospective cohort and test-negative design analyses, with the latter adjusting for age, sex, race, and other factors. The Geisinger study included adults aged 65 or older with RT-PCR-confirmed influenza between October 1, 2024, and April 30, 2025, while the VISION study covered children and adolescents aged 6 months to 17 years with ED or UC encounters for acute respiratory illness between October 1, 2025, and April 30, 2026. The VISION authors emphasized that even moderate vaccine effectiveness can significantly reduce the influenza burden in children, with the highest protection observed in those under 5 years old. The Geisinger team highlighted the need for further research on barriers to vaccination in rural populations and plans to refine their analyses using propensity score matching.
Original source text (English)
Two studies presented at IDWeek 2026 in Washington, DC, add real-world evidence for seasonal influenza vaccination in both older adults and children.1,2 A retrospective cohort study from Geisinger Health System associated vaccination with fewer ICU admissions among older adults who came to the emergency department (ED) with influenza.1 A separate analysis from the CDC’s VISION Network estimated that 2025-2026 seasonal influenza vaccines reduced the risk of influenza-associated ED and urgent care (UC) encounters among children and adolescents by 36%.2 Influenza Vaccination in Older Adults Older adults bear a disproportionate burden of severe influenza, and annual vaccination remains central to prevention.1,3 However, real-world evidence on vaccination and illness severity among ED patients is limited, according to investigators led by presenting author Manouj Kumar, MD. Geisinger serves a mostly rural, older population with substantial comorbidity, which the investigators described as a relevant setting for studying these outcomes.1 The retrospective cohort study included adults aged 65 or older who came to Geisinger EDs with RT-PCR-confirmed influenza between October 1, 2024, and April 30, 2025. Individuals were counted as vaccinated if they had received a seasonal vaccine at least 14 days before presentation, as documented in the electronic health record or immunization registry.1 Lower ICU Admission Rates Among Vaccinated Patients Of 452 patients, 80 (17.7%) were vaccinated. The 2 groups had similar baseline characteristics. Median age was 80 years in vaccinated patients and 78 years in unvaccinated patients. Chronic obstructive pulmonary disease was present in 41.3% and 33.9%, respectively.1 ICU admission occurred in 2.5% (2/80) of vaccinated patients compared with 10.5% (39/372) of unvaccinated patients. That is an 8.0-percentage-point absolute difference (P = .029). Vaccinated patients also had shorter stays, although neither difference was significant on two-sided testing:1 The mean hospital stay was 4.43 days versus 5.20 days (P = .091). Among the 41 ICU patients, the mean ICU stay was 0.67 days versus 3.24 days (P = .065). Several outcomes did not differ significantly between groups:1 Hospital admission: 95.0% vs 96.2%; Oxygen use: 75.0% in both groups; Antibiotic prescribing: 47.5% vs 45.2%; and Antiviral prescribing: 90.0% vs 87.4%. The investigators noted that only 17.7% of the cohort had documented vaccination. They said rural access and insurance-related barriers warrant further study. The analyses were unadjusted. Planned next steps include propensity score matching, analyses across multiple seasons, and evaluation of targeted electronic health record vaccination reminders.1 Pediatric Vaccine Effectiveness in the 2025-2026 Season During the 2025-2026 US influenza season, drifted influenza A (H3N2) subclade K viruses predominated, and influenza activity among children was high. To estimate vaccine effectiveness (VE) in this season, investigators led by Katherine Adams, DrPH, MPH, of the CDC’s National Center for Immunization and Respiratory Diseases, used a test-negative design. The study included children and adolescents aged 6 months to 17 years with ED or UC encounters for acute respiratory illness and molecular influenza testing at 6 VISION sites between October 1, 2025, and April 30, 2026. Models were adjusted for age, sex, race and ethnicity, calendar day, and site.2 Of 76,815 encounters, 25,496 were influenza cases and 51,319 were controls. Of the cases, 22% were vaccinated, compared with 29% of controls. Key adjusted VE estimates were as follows:2 Overall: 36% (95% CI, 33%-39%); Influenza A: 38% (95% CI, 35%-41%); Influenza B: 31% (95% CI, 27%-36%); Aged 6 months to 4 years: 47% (95% CI, 42%-51%); Aged 5 to 17 years: 29% (95% CI, 25%-33%); ED encounters: 41% (95% CI, 38%-45%); and UC encounters: 28% (95% CI, 24%-33%). VE was 40% at 14 to 59 days after vaccination, 37% at 60 to 119 days, and 34% at 120 days or more.2 Moderate Effectiveness, Meaningful Impact The VISION investigators concluded that even moderate VE can substantially reduce the influenza burden among children and adolescents. The highest protection was seen in children aged younger than 5. The Geisinger investigators said continued vaccination outreach and adjusted analyses will help translate their findings into clinical practice.1 The VISION authors noted that their findings do not necessarily represent the official position of the CDC.2 REFERENCES 1. Kumar M, Tice AM, Law JM, Kwon T, Wolk DM. Time
Two studies presented at IDWeek 2026 in Washington, DC, analyzed data from October 1, 2024, to April 30, 2025, for older adults and October 1, 2025, to April 30, 2026, for children and adolescents.
Location and population
Geisinger Health System, which serves a mostly rural, older population with substantial comorbidity, and six VISION sites across the US for pediatric analysis.
Event
Two studies presented at IDWeek 2026 found that seasonal influenza vaccination was associated with fewer severe outcomes in older adults and reduced risk of influenza-associated ED and UC encounters in children and adolescents. These findings are based on retrospective cohort and test-negative design analyses.
Scale, impact and risk
Among older adults, 17.7% were vaccinated, with 2.5% of vaccinated patients admitted to ICU compared to 10.5% of unvaccinated patients. In children, 22% of cases were vaccinated, with overall vaccine effectiveness (VE) estimated at 36% (95% CI, 33%-39%) against influenza-associated ED and UC encounters.
Response, status and uncertainty

The Geisinger investigators suggested further study on rural access and insurance-related barriers, and planned next steps include propensity score matching and analyses across multiple seasons.

B 常规重要公共卫生新闻

三价流感疫苗“够用”吗 应对今年毒株有效

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)
新闻简报该文章于 2026 年 10 月 25 日发表,讨论了三价流感 疫苗 在中国的可用性和有效性。它指出四价 疫苗 不再可用,引发了人们对三价 疫苗 是否足够的担忧。中国疾病预防控制中心的数据显示,甲型H3N2流感病毒已成为优势毒株,占所有检测到的流感病毒的80.6%,甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09和B/Victoria分别占19.4%和33.9%。文章指出,三价和四价疫苗的保护效果本质上是相同的,因为自2020年以来,B/Yamagata谱系在全球范围内尚未检测到,并且已从疫苗成分中删除。它还强调,流感季节通常从 11 月到 3 月,现在建议使用 疫苗接种,因为身体需要 2 到 4 周的时间来产生保护性抗体。该文章没有报告任何具体病例或死亡人数,但强调了 疫苗接种 在当前流感季节的重要性。
查看新闻五要素
时间
2026 年 10 月 25 日,活动日期为当前流感季节活跃期(通常为 11 月至次年 3 月)。
地点与对象
中国,重点关注因天气变冷而导致流感病毒活动增加的地区。
事件
本文讨论了三价流感 疫苗 的可用性及其对抗今年主要流感病毒株(包括 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09、甲型H3N2流感病毒 和 B/Victoria)的有效性。它提到四价 疫苗 不再可用,引发了关于三价 疫苗 是否足够的问题。
规模、影响与风险
文章报告称,甲型H3N2流感病毒 占所有检测到的流感病毒的 80.6%,而 甲型H1N1流感病毒pdm09 和 B/Victoria 分别占 19.4% 和 33.9%。其指出,三价和四价疫苗的保护作用本质上是相同的,没有提及具体病例或死亡人数。
应对、状态与不确定性
文章指出,当前流感季节活跃,建议立即接种疫苗。它还指出,世界卫生组织建议从 疫苗 组件中删除 B/Yamagata 谱系,因为它自 2020 年以来就不再存在。来源是一篇完整的文章,因此没有提及任何限制。

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

Original title (Chinese)
三价流感疫苗“够用”吗 应对今年毒株有效
News Brief
The article, published on October 25, 2026, discusses the availability and effectiveness of trivalent influenza vaccines in China. It notes that quadrivalent vaccines are no longer available, raising concerns about whether trivalent vaccines are sufficient. According to data from the Chinese Center for Disease Control and Prevention, A(H3N2) has become the dominant strain, accounting for 80.6% of all influenza viruses detected, while A(H1N1)pdm09 and B/Victoria account for 19.4% and 33.9%, respectively. The article states that the protection effect of trivalent and quadrivalent vaccines is essentially the same, as the B/Yamagata lineage has not been detected globally since 2020 and has been removed from vaccine components. It also highlights that the flu season typically runs from November to March, and vaccination is recommended now as it takes 2 to 4 weeks for the body to develop protective antibodies. The article does not report any specific case or death counts but emphasizes the importance of vaccination during the current flu season.

Time
2026-10-25, with the event date being the current period as the flu season is active (typically 11月至次年3月).
Location and population
China, with the focus on regions where flu virus activity has increased due to cooling weather.
Event
The article discusses the availability of trivalent influenza vaccines and their effectiveness against this year's dominant flu strains, which include A(H1N1)pdm09, A(H3N2), and B/Victoria. It mentions that quadrivalent vaccines are no longer available, leading to questions about whether trivalent vaccines are sufficient.
Scale, impact and risk
The article reports that A(H3N2) accounts for 80.6% of all influenza viruses detected, while A(H1N1)pdm09 and B/Victoria account for 19.4% and 33.9%, respectively. It states that the protection effect of trivalent and quadrivalent vaccines is essentially the same, and no specific case or death counts are mentioned.
Response, status and uncertainty
The article indicates that the current flu season is active, and it is recommended to get vaccinated now. It also notes that the World Health Organization has advised removing the B/Yamagata lineage from vaccine components due to its absence since 2020. The source is a full article, so no limitations are mentioned.

顶部 首页 首页 晴 / 14° 隐私 事关流感,中疾控最新发布→ 新浪财经 (来源:新密发布) 新密市各镇卫生院预防接种门诊 💉💉💉 今年接种流感疫苗时 深入探索 搜狐招聘服务 高校招聘会 价值公司联盟 不少人发现四价疫苗不见了 只有三价疫苗 难道是疫苗“降级”了? 三价疫苗能否应对今年的毒株? 展开剩余 69 % 今年流行什么毒株? 据中国疾控中心监测显示,今年7月以来,全国甲型H3N2亚型和乙型Victoria系共同流行,甲型H3N2亚型占比上升,已超过乙型Victoria系。 中国疾控中心发布的流感监测周报进一步反映了这一流行特征: 南、北方省份流感病毒检测阳性率略有上升,全国检出流感阳性样本中甲型流感病毒占比66.1%、乙型流感病毒占比33.9%; 甲型流感病毒中A(H1N1)pdm09占比19.4%、A(H3N2)占比80.6%,乙型流感病毒均为B(Victoria)系。 专家提示,国内的流感高发期通常是11月到次年的3月。接种流感疫苗后,人体需要2到4周才能产生足够的保护性抗体,因此眼下是流感疫苗黄金接种期。 三价流感疫苗“够用”吗? 2026—2027年度,我国上市的三价流感疫苗包括三价裂解疫苗、三价亚单位疫苗和三价减毒活疫苗。 其中三价裂解疫苗和亚单位疫苗可以用于6月龄及以上的人群,包括0.25毫升和0.5毫升两种规格。 三价减毒活疫苗适用于3—17岁的人群,每剂次0.2毫升。 专家提醒,目前上市的流感疫苗均能有效预防流感,不用刻意追求价格、品牌、剂型,尽早完成接种最重要。 哪些人该打流感疫苗? 事关流感,中疾控最新发布

News article (Chinese original; verified English title unavailable)

由于乙型Yamagata系自2020年3月以来在全球再未被监测到,世界卫生组织建议将其从疫苗组分中移除。2026至2027年度,世卫组织不再提供乙型Yamagata系的推荐毒株,全球流感疫苗全面调整为三价疫苗。四价疫苗较三价疫苗在当前流行形势下,不具备更多优势,两种疫苗保护效果基本一致。
